**D104.** Tool-block renderers share three consistency conventions, upheld by authorship and review rather than enforced as tuglaws (they are implementation facts, not locked-in project law). **(1) Status is the dot's; data is the badge's.** Lifecycle (`completed` / `running` / `failed` / `deleted`) is carried by the header's pulsing dot alone ([D02]); a renderer never paints lifecycle text in the detail column or a body field row. The one trailing `resultSummary` badge slot carries *data* — a compact token (`936 lines`, `19 calls`, `exit 0`, `deleted`) — never a sentence; sentence-shaped domain output stays in the body under an honest label (e.g. `result`, not `status`). **(2) One fold per block.** The chrome's whole-block chevron is the single fold whenever the block is collapsible; a body kind shows its own fold cue only where there is no chrome chevron (a nested tool dispatched at `depth + 1`, with no `ToolBlockHistoryCollapse` wrapper). The signal is `BlockFoldSuppressedContext` — truthy means the chrome owns the fold, so the body suppresses its cue. **(3) Streaming body is empty; the dot is the signal.** Per [D02] a streaming tool passes `body = null`; there is no shared `StreamingPlaceholder` (it was specified but never built, and the docstrings that named it were swept). The lone exception is the Agent block, whose `AgentWorkingBody` fills its uniquely long working window with calm, non-animated content (not a competing status signal). Originating plan: [D02], [D05], [L06], [L20]

**D108.** **`/btw` side questions are driven by claude's native `side_question` control-request, rendered overlay-only, and are invisible to the transcript and to replay.** A side question — ask claude something about the current work, answered from the full conversation with no tools — is *not* a text-expanding slash command (that path claude refuses headless, `num_turns:0`); it is a `control_request { subtype: "side_question", question }`, serviced by the same inbound handler as `initialize`/`interrupt`/`set_model`. Probe-verified serviced **idle AND mid-turn** on claude 2.1.204 (`tugcode/probes/btw/FINDINGS.md`); the answer is a single settled `control_response` (`response.response.{response,synthetic}`), preceded by one `system/control_request_progress{started}` frame — not token-streamed. Three rules make it work and keep it honest: **(1) Tug is transport + surface, not semantics ([P01]).** claude's `runSideQuestion` owns the system-reminder framing, tool suppression, cache reuse, and `threadHistory:false` history exclusion; tugcode only forwards the control-request and correlates the turn-free response into a `side_question_answer` frame (caught in `handleClaudeLine` *before* turn routing, beside `initialize`/rewind — which is why a mid-turn response never corrupts the streaming turn). **(2) Overlay-only, never transcript ink ([P02]/[P05]).** The answer renders in a non-modal `TugPopover` (never a pane-modal `TugSheet`, which would `inert` the transcript and defeat mid-turn) anchored at the prompt entry. `side_question_answer` is deliberately **absent from `KNOWN_CODE_OUTPUT_TYPES`**, so the code-session store drops it — it reaches only the dedicated per-session `SideQuestionStore` feed. Nothing about the exchange lands on `state.transcript`, so a Maker ▸ Reload (which re-resumes from JSONL, where side questions never appear) reconstructs an identical transcript. **(3) Ephemeral, session-scoped ([P03]).** `SideQuestionStore` holds the ask/answer history in memory only (no tugbank/JSONL/web storage), cleared on `/clear` and card teardown. `/btw` is `supported-local` and inherits mid-turn dispatch for free — local commands dispatch before the send-readiness gate, so no `performSubmit` change is needed ([P04]). Originating plan: [L02], [L06], [L26] **Addendum ([D110]):** `/btw` gains a second entry point — the `?` **route** in the Z4A choice group. A submission on the `?` route dispatches the same `/btw <text>` local command through the same `RUN_SLASH_COMMAND` responder path, so the overlay-only / never-transcript-ink / ephemeral rules above hold identically; the route is just a second door to this one surface.

**D110.** *(Superseded by [D122] — the three-recipient route model is retired; the `$` route's shell backend survives under [D111], reached now by the auto-router and `/shell`, and `?` btw survives as the `/btw` command. Kept for the history of how the group got here.)* The Session card's Z4A route choice group carries **three routes — `❯` Code, `$` Shell, `?` btw — modelled as *recipients*** of a submission: Code = Claude on the record (the transcript), Shell = the machine, btw = Claude off the record (a native side question, [D108]). Each route owns its Return semantics (`RETURN_ACTION_BY_ROUTE`) and a `SELECT_ROUTE` keybinding (⇧⌘C / ⇧⌘S / ⇧⌘B). (The prefix-alias trigger — typing the route char at offset 0 flipped the route — was later removed; route characters are ordinary text.) ⇧⌘C required freeing the chord from `Copy as Plain Text` (moved to ⌥⇧⌘C, paired with `Paste as Plain Text` at ⌥⇧⌘V) in **both** the web keymap (`keybinding-map.ts`, whose exact-modifier matcher then lets a bare ⇧⌘C fall through to the route) and the Swift Edit menu (`AppDelegate.swift`, which owns the chord at the macOS menu bar). **Per-route submit dispatch** lives in `performSubmit` (`tug-prompt-entry.tsx`): `❯` → `codeSessionStore.send` (unchanged); `?` → intercept BEFORE the local-command split (the route IS the recipient, not the leading token) and dispatch `/btw <question>` via `RUN_SLASH_COMMAND`, recording the RAW question in history (not the synthesized `/btw` line) — inherits mid-turn dispatch for free ([D108] [P04]); `$` → an interim `SHOW_SLASH_COMMAND_NOTICE` (`shell-pending`) that preserves the draft rather than silently sending the command to Claude (the shell backend lands in a later phase, Phase 3). **Per-route Z4B chrome** is a declarative manifest (`SessionRouteChromeManifest`, Table T01) replacing the old `SessionRouteShellGate` disable-scatter: `❯` shows identity(Claude Code) · Mode · Model · Effort; `$` shows identity(Shell) · Project · Cwd; `?` shows identity(Claude Code) · Session · Project. (The code route dropped its Session and Project chips when the card was put on a diet for the slim width preset: both names already stand in the pane title bar via `sessionCardTitleOverride`, so the row was saying them twice, and the code route is the one that runs out of width. Shell and commit are not space-challenged and keep theirs — [D128].) Chips a route drops **unmount** (show/hide, not disabled), and the focus cycle skips them for free; the identity badge never unmounts ([L26]) and stable keys preserve mount identity for chips that survive a flip (Project on every route, Session on code + btw). Swapping the centred-floating Z4B occupant on an explicit route gesture is the slot working as designed ([D97] — Z4B occupancy is a layout decision, not contract); the leading Z4A group and trailing Z5 submit stay edge-pinned across the swap. The `Cwd` chip (a shipped path chip alongside `Project`) shows the shell session's working directory; until the shell backend exists it falls back to the project dir. Originating plan: ([P01]/[P02]/[P03]). [D97], [D108], [L02], [L06], [L26] **Addendum ([D111]):** Phase 3 replaced the `$`-route interim notice with the real block-oriented shell backend and bound the `Cwd` chip live to the shell session — the recipient model and Z4B manifest above stand unchanged; the recipient is now wired to a running shell.

**D111.** *(The `$` **route** is retired by [D122]; everything below about the shell BACKEND stands unchanged — only the door moved, from a route to the auto-router plus an explicit `/shell`.)* **The `$` route runs a real block-oriented shell whose command exchanges thread into the transcript as durable, visually-distinct *non-context ink* — the transcript is the session's record of what happened, not a strict mirror of Claude's context ([P11]).** A `$`-route submission is executed by a per-session persistent POSIX-shell child in tugcast (`feeds/shell.rs`): **the user's own `$SHELL` (or `/bin/zsh`), spawned interactive-login (`-il`)** so it sources their startup files — PATH, exports, aliases, functions — and the route feels like their terminal, not a bare shell; spawned in pipe mode under `setsid` (no controlling TTY, so `/dev/tty` grabs fail fast and TUIs decline), running each command as `{ …; } </dev/null` with `exec 2>&1` so stdout+stderr merge, framed by a sentinel (`printf '\n<sentinel>\t%d\t%s\n' "$?" "$PWD"`) read until it appears — settled-whole output for v1, with a per-exchange timeout backstop that reaps a wedged group (`kill(-pgid)`) and respawns fresh in the project dir. Interactive mode brings baggage a block shell must not leak, all neutralized by a **post-startup preamble** (run after rc sourcing so it wins): `trap - TERM QUIT` restores the SIGTERM disposition an interactive shell ignores (else the reap can't kill it), the precmd/preexec hooks and `promptsp` marker are cleared, and every prompt parameter is emptied; a **discarded warmup exchange** then absorbs any rc-file chatter so real exchanges start clean. A profile that hangs or fails the warmup can never brick the route — the child is reaped and a plain rc-less shell takes its place, flagged once in the next exchange's output. **No TTY emulator**: the block model is deliberate, not a terminal. The child persists across exchanges, so `cd` is stateful and the live `Cwd` chip ([D110], [P10]) tracks it. **The deck side is a clean split of ownership ([P12]):** `ShellSessionStore` (one per card) is the sole consumer of the `SHELL_OUTPUT` feed and owns *session* state (`live` / `cwd` / the in-flight exchange that drives the route-aware Z5 `stop` pose, [P13]); it does NOT own the rows — each exchange's transcript presence lives in `CodeSessionStore`, fed via `ingestShellExchange` on `exchange_started` (mint an in-flight `shell`-origin turn) and `exchange_complete` (settle it in place). `SHELL_OUTPUT` is deliberately absent from the code-session feed filter, so there is one consumer chain and no dual-ingest race; the `exchange_complete` frame is self-contained (it re-carries command/cwd/started_at/settled_at) so the settle — live and on restore — needs no correlation with an earlier frame. A `shell`-origin `TurnEntry` holds a single `shell_exchange` message and is disjoint from the Claude phase / activeTurn / scratch machinery — it renders as `#s` (`turn-metric.md` S01/S02) inside a `participant="shell"` transcript entry, styled as subdued non-context ink so it never reads as Claude context. **The exchange body reuses the standard tool-block chrome for family consistency, not a bespoke layout:** `ShellExchangeBlock` composes the shared `BlockChrome` with the command in the header (the `$` sigil + command, exactly where `BashToolBlock` puts its command) and a right-aligned Add-to-context + Copy cluster, over an `embedded` `TerminalBlock` for the output; the exit status + duration are NOT drawn inside the block but on a Z1B end-state row beneath it (`SessionZ1B participant="shell"` — the exit badge where a Claude turn shows OK/Error, reusing `endStateBadgeFor`'s grammar via `shellEndStateBadge`). The block carries no tool NAME (the `Shell` row identity already names it; a repeat would be redundant, unlike a `Bash` block under the shared `Code` identity). **Persistence + restore ([P07]):** every settled exchange is recorded to a per-session `ShellLedger` (`shell_exchanges.db`, capped 500/session, oldest evicted) beside `sessions.db`; a Maker ▸ Reload / relaunch re-fetches via the `list_shell_exchanges` → `list_shell_exchanges_ok` CONTROL pair (never feed replay) and mints each row keyed `restored-<sqlite-id>`, interleaving by the exchange's `started_at_ms`. **Interleave is one-directional by design, and the reason is empirical:** shell turns upsert into the append-ordered Claude spine by timestamp (`upsertShellTurn`), and a Claude append slides past trailing shell turns the restore seated ahead of it (`appendTurnInterleavingShell`) — but the Claude stream itself is NEVER timestamp-sorted, because the real on-disk JSONL corpus is ~39% non-monotonic in entry timestamp (sidechains, `--continue`/`--resume` merges, and compaction re-insert older stamps mid-stream). JSONL arrival order is the source of truth for Claude turns; timestamp is a reliable key only for shell turns (a ledger `started_at_ms`) and genuine user submissions (a `submitAt` from the JSONL) — exactly the pair the interleave sorts on. **The interleave also holds for submissions that have not committed yet:** an in-flight turn and the queued-send ghosts live outside the committed transcript, so the row layout merges the transcript's trailing shell rows INTO that pending block by timestamp (`composeRowLayout`), under the same rule the commit will apply — otherwise an exchange the user ran *after* hitting submit paints above their own message for the length of the turn and then jumps. A queued send is stamped when the user submits it, not when the queue flushes (`QueuedSend.queuedAt`), so the shell rows run during its wait stay below it permanently. **Claude never sees a shell exchange implicitly ([P08]):** the only path into Claude's context is the on-demand **Add to context** toggle on a settled exchange row — it composes a fenced `command + output + exit` block (oversized output truncated at a line boundary; the fence lengthens past any backtick run) and stages it on the pending-context queue, where it rides the next `❯` submission as attributed `#s{n}` context. Never auto-sent, and a second click un-stages it. **One gesture, one glyph:** `cloud-upload` marks add-to-context everywhere it appears (shell rows and the `/btw` overlay alike), going `check` while staged. The earlier **Share** affordance — a second, redundant path that seeded the composer directly — is retired along with its `requestShare`/`consumePendingShare` slot; two ways to do one thing is what made the header read as a cluster of near-synonyms. **Richness accretes without a rewrite:** shell rows resolve their renderer through a command-block registry on the [D101] grammar (`session-command-block-registry.ts` — `registerCommandBlock(name, matcher, renderer)` / `resolveCommandBlock(command)`, total default to the generic exchange block, throw on duplicate name); no bespoke renderers ship (`git status` / `ls` / build-progress blocks are follow-ons, [P05]). Probe: `tugrust/crates/tugcast/probes/shell-exec/FINDINGS.md`. Originating plan: ([P04]–[P13], Milestone M03). [D97], [D101], [D108], [D110], [L02], [L06], [L26]

---

## Dev Prompt Entry

**D98.** Host facts — the backend's network `hostname` and the basename of its login shell — are resolved by tugcast and served at a read-only `GET /api/host` endpoint, then read into tugdeck's `HostFactsStore` through `useSyncExternalStore`. The browser cannot know the backend's real hostname or `$SHELL` (`window.location.hostname` is only the URL host), and the facts are static for a server's lifetime, so the store fires the fetch exactly once and caches the result. The endpoint is loopback-restricted like the other `/api` handlers; the response shape `{ hostname, shell }` is a cross-stack contract, pinned by a Rust serialization test and a `bun:test` parser test. A failed or pending fetch leaves the store empty, and consumers treat an empty snapshot as "not yet known." [L02]

**D99.** Each Dev prompt entry owns a `RouteLifecycle` — a per-prompt-entry pipe that holds the authoritative command route (`❯` Code / `$` Shell) and announces every change. It offers two surfaces over one fire path: a store surface (`subscribe` + `getRoute`) that renderers read through `useSyncExternalStore` ([L02]), and a synchronous delegate/observer surface (`observeRouteWillChange` / `observeRouteDidChange`, `useRouteDelegate`) for imperative reactors. The route is no longer component `useState`: once it has a consumer outside the component that owns it, it is external state and must enter React through `useSyncExternalStore`. Unlike the deck-level `CardLifecycle`, `RouteLifecycle` is scoped per prompt entry, provided through `RouteLifecycleContext`, surfaces a single `(prev, next)` will/did pair, and dispatches synchronously — route consumers re-render content and have no gesture-focus-lock hazard, so there is no `MessageChannel` drain. See [route-lifecycle.md](route-lifecycle.md). [L02], [L03]

---

## Onboarding & Setup

**D106.** ConfigureTug — the app-modal onboarding wizard (`configure-tug.tsx`, [#step-9] of) — renders as an ordered list of **step rows**, each a **bespoke row** (not a transcript `BlockChrome` [D104]): a left-hand `TugProgressIndicator variant="pulsing-dot"` whose role+state encode the step's lifecycle ([D02]), a requirement/direction **label**, a **detail** line carrying state/progress/completion, and an optional **CTA**. The block shell was evaluated and rejected for this surface — its tool-name/result-summary/copy/chevron affordances are transcript baggage a setup step does not want; the bespoke row keeps the wizard's own rhythm. A step's status is one of `pending | active | busy | error | done`, mapping to the dot as: `pending`→`inherit/stopped` (row dimmed), `active`→`action/paused`, `busy`→`agent/running`, `error`→`danger/aborted`, `done`→`success/completed`. The dot breathes only while work is in flight: the pulse means activity, not the user's turn, so a row waiting on the user wears a full, still blue dot and only `busy` animates. The pulsing dot is the *only* role tone on the row; the label/detail inherit panel text ([D104] convention 1 — status is the dot's). The design spike that fixes this surface's copy and rhythm without a clean guest is the **`gallery-configure-tug`** card (`gallery-configure-tug.tsx`), which simulates every state below from local state — it never touches the real `authStore`.

**Flow.** Four steps, each gated on the one before, fed by the app-level `authStore` ([L02]), the `claudeVersionStore` version pair, and the deck card count — and above them the git row [D171], which is gated on nothing, blocks nothing, and disappears for good once skipped:

```
   (first launch) ──▶ PROBING  "Checking your setup…"
                          │
   GIT ROW ── Install git   ([D171]; skippable, and gone for good once skipped)
     active (Install / Skip for now) ─▶ busy (Recheck) ─▶ done ✓
                                    └─▶ error (Retry) ─┘
                          │
   STEP 1 ── Install Claude Code   (label flips to "Claude Code installed" on done)
     active (Install) ─▶ busy (Installing…) ─▶ done ✓ / done + Update
                    └─▶ error (Retry) ─┘        └─▶ busy (Updating…) ─▶ done ✓
                                                └─▶ error (Retry) ─┘
                          │ done
   STEP 2 ── Log in to Claude
     active (Log In) ─▶ busy (Logging in…) ─▶ done ✓
                    └─▶ error (Try Again) ─┘
                          │ done
   STEP 3 ── Choose a default project directory
     active (Choose) ─▶ busy (Creating…) ─▶ done ✓
                   └─▶ error (Retry) ─┘
                          │ done
   STEP 4 ── Start a session   (success/transition button)
     active (Start) ─▶ opens first card ─▶ wizard dismisses

   Transport down (replaces body): a "Reconnecting…" step row
   Sibling app-modal (wins):       VERSION TOO OLD → TugVersionGate ([#step-7])
```

**Per-state copy (what we show & say).** Label is the requirement/direction; detail is the state/progress/completion line. A `busy` step keeps a **disabled** CTA (not hidden) so the row doesn't empty out; a `done` step swaps the CTA for a green success check (✓) — **unless it carries an offer anyway** (the installed-but-behind row), where the outlined CTA takes the check's slot: the dot already says settled, and a check beside an Update button would be two answers to one question.

**Version, and updating.** Step 1 carries the whole life of the install, not just its presence. `check_claude_version` (sent beside `check_auth` at launch/reconnect, and again on an on-demand open — a launch-time answer goes stale) answers `claude_version_result {installed, latest}`: `claude --version` locally, and the **stable release channel** (`downloads.claude.ai/claude-code-releases/stable`) for what is on offer — the same channel the installer lands, so "up to date" means up to date with what pressing Update would do. Either half may be `null` (no CLI, no network) and the row then says only what it knows; the Update CTA appears only when both are known and `compareVersions` proves the local one older, so a locally built or ahead-of-channel `claude` never reads as behind. **The updater IS the installer** — the official installer always lands the newest stable build — so `update_claude` reuses the install shell-out and differs only in what the row says. A successful update stays `busy` until the version re-probe lands, so the row never flashes the old version back as if nothing happened. State lives in `claudeVersionStore` ([L02]), deliberately apart from `authStore`: the picker gate and per-card banner read auth and have no business re-rendering on a version lookup.

**Logged out on a configured app.** The Log Out gesture (and any relaunch with the login revoked) reopens this wizard with **only steps 1 and 2**. The directory is already chosen and the session step is answered by the deck the user is returning to, so the other two rows would be settled state the user cannot act on while logged out — the wizard shows only what it is actually asking. The condition is a stored default project directory plus a logged-out state; a genuine first run (nothing stored) still gets the whole checklist.

| Step | Status | Dot (role/state) | Label | Detail | CTA |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| — | probing | agent / running | Install Claude Code | "Looking for Claude Code…" | — |
| 1 | active | action / running | Install Claude Code | "Tug will install it for you." | **Install** |
| 1 | busy | agent / running | Install Claude Code | "This can take a moment." | *Installing…* (disabled) |
| 1 | error | danger / aborted | Install Claude Code | "Install failed: \<error\>" | **Retry** |
| 1 | done (current) | success / completed | Claude Code installed | "Version \<installed\> — up to date." | ✓ |
| 1 | done (channel unknown) | success / completed | Claude Code installed | "Version \<installed\>" | ✓ |
| 1 | done (behind) | success / completed | Claude Code installed | "Version \<installed\> — \<latest\> is available." | **Update** (outlined) |
| 1 | busy (update) | agent / running | Update Claude Code | "Installing \<latest\>…" | *Updating…* (disabled) |
| 1 | error (update) | danger / aborted | Update Claude Code | "Update failed: \<error\>" | **Retry** |
| 2 | active | action / running | Log in to Claude | "Tug runs sessions with your Claude subscription." | **Log In** |
| 2 | busy | agent / running | Log in to Claude | "Use your browser to log in…" | *Logging in…* (disabled) |
| 2 | error | danger / aborted | Log in to Claude | "Log-in didn't finish. The browser may have been closed." | **Try Again** |
| 2 | done | success / completed | Logged in as \<email\> | "Claude \<Tier\> plan" | ✓ |
| 3 | active | action / running | Choose a default project directory | "Tug opens new sessions in this directory by default." | **Choose** (on the chooser's own line) |
| 3 | busy | agent / running | Choose a default project directory | "Creating the folder…" | *Creating…* (disabled) |
| 3 | error | danger / aborted | Choose a default project directory | "Couldn't create \<path\>." | **Retry** |
| 3 | done | success / completed | Default project directory | \<path\> | ✓ |
| 4 | pending (no dir yet) | inherit / stopped | Start a session | "Waiting for a default project directory." | — |
| 4 | pending (cards open) | inherit / stopped | Continue working | "You'll return to your \<N\> open cards." | — |
| 4 | active | action / running | Start a session | "Start working in a new session." | **Start** |
| 4 | done | success / completed | Start a session | "Opening Session card…" | ✓ |
| — | transport down | agent / running | Reconnecting… | "Lost the connection to Tug. Setup will resume automatically." | — |

The step-4 pending row previews the return to work while the user is still logged out (the "Continue working" case, [P04] of): with cards open, re-login auto-closes the wizard straight back to them (the `open` derivation is `notReady || needsFirstSession`, which goes false the instant a logged-in deck has cards), so the preview lives in the logged-out window rather than as an active step. The `pendingOpenStepCopy` helper (`configure-tug-copy.ts`, unit-tested) owns the label/detail branch on card count.

**Verbiage.** Tug uses the consistent **"Log in" / "Log out"** pair for the account action (the `claude` CLI itself says "Sign in"/"Log out" — inconsistent; Tug does not follow it). The `subscriptionLabel` helper (`configure-tug-copy.ts`, unit-tested) formalizes the tier as "Claude Max plan" etc. **Logout** reopens this same wizard: an app-level "Log out…" (File menu + `/logout`) confirms via TugAlert, interrupts every in-progress turn **first** (each tagged `interrupt("logout")` so its Z1B end-state reads "Stopped — logged out"), then runs `claude_logout`, and flips `authStore` logged-out so ConfigureTug returns to the Log-in step (a failed/timed-out logout surfaces a "Couldn't log out" alert and leaves the user logged in). The `TugLogout` orchestrator (deck-root sibling) owns that flow.

**One login surface.** ConfigureTug is the *only* login UI. There is no per-card in-card sign-in: when a card's session hits the auth gate (`auth_required` / `claude_missing`), the observer re-probes `check_auth` (flipping `authStore` logged-out → ConfigureTug opens app-modal) and unbinds the card to its picker with a `signed_out` notice, so the user re-logs-in once in the wizard and resumes per card from the picker. See 

**Resolved design questions.** **(1) "Open your first session" is a success/transition button, not a probed step** — its CTA opens the first Session card and the wizard dismisses. That first card seeds its **Project Path to the user's home directory** (a sensible from-the-drop default; the user re-points it afterward). **(2) The step row is bespoke** (above) — `BlockChrome` was evaluated and rejected. **(3) Probing / first-launch:** on a user's *first* launch ConfigureTug shows **up front and immediately** (it does not wait to probe behind a blank deck). This is governed by a persisted **first-launch flag** stored via tugbank defaults (`/api/defaults/…`, never `localStorage`); the flag is set once the user has launched the first time, so subsequent launches fall through to the normal probe-then-decide path. **(4) Transport-down** replaces the wizard body with a calm "Reconnecting…" takeover rather than a dead wizard; **version-too-old** is the sibling app-modal `TugVersionGate` ([#step-7], Spec S02) which takes precedence over ConfigureTug. The unhappy-path states (install-fail, sign-in cancel/timeout, transport-down) are designed in [#step-10]; this decision fixes their copy and visual grammar. [D02], [D104], [L02], [L06]

**D171.** **Tug ships no git, and it detects the machine's git in an order that must not be simplified: `PATH` first, `xcode-select -p` second, and `git --version` only once one of those has made it safe.** Tug shells out to `git` 255 times — more than to every other external binary combined — and nothing in the product ever checked that git was there, so a fresh Mac met the gap as whatever error the feature that reached for git happened to phrase: no Changes shade, no commit surface, no arcs, and nothing anywhere saying why. Bundling git the way `tmux` is bundled would work — it links only system libraries once `NO_GETTEXT`/`NO_PERL` are set, and `tugrust/scripts/fetch-tmux.sh` is a working template — and is refused anyway, on licensing rather than difficulty: git is GPLv2-only, Tug takes on no GPL obligations including §3's source offer, and every other bundled component is permissive (tmux/ISC, libevent/BSD, ncurses, utf8proc/MIT). That line holds cleanly here because the GPL program can arrive from Apple, installed by the user, from Apple's servers. Tug only points, with `xcode-select --install`. **The probe order is the part a later simplification will collapse back into a bare `git --version`, so it is recorded here as well as in `tugcore::host_tools`.** On a Mac with no active developer directory `/usr/bin/git` is not git at all — it is Apple's shim, byte-identical to `/usr/bin/clang` and hardlinked under every developer-tool name — and its whole job is to pop the "install the command line developer tools?" system modal. So a naive probe throws a dialog in the user's face before the wizard has said a word, and would be wrong as often as it was rude, because a machine carrying Homebrew git and no Command Line Tools has a perfectly good git. Hence the three steps: resolve `git` on `PATH`, and anything other than `/usr/bin/git` is a real git — version it and stop, the developer-directory question never coming up; only the shim, or nothing on `PATH` at all, reaches `xcode-select -p`, which is silent and exits 2 when no developer directory is active; and only its exit 0 makes `git --version` safe to run. Then compare against the 2.23 floor — `git switch` and `git restore`, both used — because presence is not enough: a 2.19 git otherwise fails inside an arc rather than at the door. Three consequences are part of the decision. **The wizard's git row is deferrable and the skip is remembered** — ~3 GB is too much to demand before a user has seen the app work once, and plenty of first sessions are a chat in a scratch directory, so **Skip for now** persists under `dev.tugapp.app` and the row does not return. **That is only honest because the surfaces that genuinely need git carry the same offer**: `TugNoGitNotice` in the Changes and History shades, taking precedence over `TugNonRepoNotice` (a machine with no git cannot `git init` anything, so Initialize there would be a button that cannot work), and a preflight on the `tugtool arc` verbs that refuses by name in prose, since the CLI has no button. **And the row settles by watching, not polling** — Apple's installer is a GUI the backend cannot await, and a timer asking "is git here yet?" every two seconds is the polling the house forbids, so a watch on `/Library/Developer/CommandLineTools` re-probes when the tools land and a **Recheck** CTA covers a missed watch or a manual install. `xcode-select --install` exiting 1 with "already installed" is success and must never be surfaced as an error; it is the normal answer on a configured machine. Two things are explicitly *not* decided here. Replacing the git CLI with `gix` or libgit2 is a genuine long-term option and would not remove the need for any of this, since a user's own typed `git` in a Session card would still have nothing to run. And no other host tool is checked: the survey found exactly one gap, `just`/`jq`/`bun`/`gh` are checkout-only, and a project's own build command is per-project and belongs to `tugtool arc doctor`, not to a launch-time wizard that runs before any project is chosen.

---

## Changesets & arcs

**D112.** **Attribution is recorded at the point of change, never reconstructed — and it records provenance only, never judgments.** File authorship for a session is captured in a `file_events` table in `sessions.db` from tugcast's `agent_bridge` relay loop — the one supervised point every tool frame already flows through. Write/Edit/NotebookEdit attribute exactly from tool inputs on a successful `tool_result` — with the row's `project_dir` resolved **per file** from the file's own directory (worktree-aware `file_repo_root`: repo membership is a per-file fact, never the session's project dir, which misfiled every arc session's worktree edits as `.tug/worktrees/…` under the main checkout, invisible to every reader — and a file belonging to **no** repo is not recorded at all, since the fold matches only git's repo-relative dirty paths and such a row could never surface anywhere); `Bash` needs working-tree fingerprint bracketing (snapshot on `tool_use`, delta on `tool_result`); and the **turn-scoped fallback bracket** (`origin='turn'`, `tool_name='Turn'`) snapshots on the `user_message` forward and takes the delta on that turn's non-replayed `turn_complete`, attributing any path no exact/per-call-Bash row already covered — closing the capture gaps a per-call Bash bracket structurally cannot (its pre-snapshot racing a fast command, or a relay crash mid-Bash within a live turn). **All brackets are relay-local**; there is no cross-session bracket registry, and no capture path ever observes or marks another session. The retired alternative — a shared registry cross-marking rows `ambiguous` on repo-root + wall-clock overlap between sessions' brackets — was wrong twice over: unrelated concurrent commands on one checkout false-positived constantly (with several session cards open, essentially everything went ambiguous), and the hazard it aimed at (two sessions writing the same file, each bracket catching the other's delta) *always* manifests as both sessions holding rows for that path anyway. The correct signal is therefore **per-file contention, computed at read time**, and gated on proof: a file is `shared` iff two or more sessions hold **live proof rows** (`exact`/`replay` — the tool input names the file) for that exact repo-relative path. Proof decides ownership; `bash`/`turn` bracket rows are a whole-tree fingerprint *delta* that cannot separate this session's writes from a concurrent save, build churn, **or the user's own hand-save in their editor** (which belongs to no session and can never be claimed back) — so correlation never decides any bucket, **not even for the bracket's own session**: a bracket-only file stays `unattributed`, tagged `likely this session's (bash bracket)` as a hint, and inclusion is the same explicit disposition as any other unattributed file. (Each weaker rule reintroduced the bug through a side door: "any row = authorship" let two sessions' `cargo`/`git` brackets sweep up each other's saves so everything went `shared`; "a bracket attributes its own session when no exact owner exists" would let an agent's bracket auto-commit the user's inflight hand-saves.) A row is live only while it postdates the last commit that touched its path (a commit *spends* the rows it absorbs — a fossil row neither attributes nor contends when the file is re-dirtied later; ties break toward spent, degrading to `unattributed`, visible). The legacy `ambiguous` column remains in the schema but is always written 0 and read by nothing. The ledger itself is **machine-global**: one `changes.db` (`tugcore::instance::changes_db_path`, independent of `TUG_INSTANCE_ID`; `TUG_CHANGES_DB` is a test-isolation override) attached by every instance's `SessionLedger` — the working tree is machine-global, so per-instance attribution splits the truth (a second app instance on the same checkout saw the first's work as ownerless and its card pre-selected the lot; both halves are fixed — shared ledger + unattributed-defaults-off). All three source rules (exact, `bash`, `turn`) hold the same invariants: point-of-change capture, an upsert keyed `(tug_session_id, tool_use_id, file_path)` so replay/resume converges idempotently, and attribution failures never affecting frame delivery (degrade to "forward unchanged"). Shared rows are excluded from every default commit set (the card's selection and `tugtool commit` alike, opt-in via `--all`/`--tree`/`--paths`) — one session's commit never silently sweeps another's file. The commit *read* side treats no row as a gate: `tugchanges-core`'s join is inverted so `git status` is the universe and the ledger annotates it (a dirty file is never invisible; see). Full doctrine: [tracking-changes.md](tracking-changes.md). 

**D113.** **The changeset surface is a process-level aggregate feed, not per-workspace.** `CHANGESET_ALL` (a single process-level snapshot watch, the `spawn_stats_feeds`/`defaults` pattern) aggregates every open project into one `WorkspacesChangesetSnapshot` delivered to every deck, because tugdeck's `FeedStore` holds one value per feed id — a second project's frame on the same id would overwrite the first, so aggregation must be server-side. The project set is exactly the current `WorkspaceRegistry` entries (one per project with a live session card); non-repo dirs surface with an "Initialize git" affordance rather than being skipped. The retired per-workspace `CHANGESET` (0x23) stays reserved, never reused. The card retires the old Git card entirely. 

**D114.** **Dash is its own tool, `tugdash`, not a `tugtool` subcommand.** The worktree-isolated work-unit engine lives in `tugarc-core` (library, callable from tugcast) with a `tugdash` CLI over it (`create`/`commit`/`join`/`release`/`list`/`show`), so the Changeset card drives create/commit/join through the same code as the CLI. An arc *is* a `tugdash/<name>` git branch plus a worktree under `<repo>/.tug/worktrees/<name>` (auto-migrated from the legacy `.tugtree/tugdash__<name>` when clean and unheld); its base branch and description live in git config, its activity in the per-project append-only arc log — there is no database. `tugtool dash` is deleted; the tugplug skills and docs speak `tugdash`. **Superseded twice.** The standalone `tugdash` binary folded back into `tugtool` as the `arc` verb group, and the work unit is now spelled **arc** everywhere a person reads it: the branch prefix is `tugarc/<name>`, migrated at the top of every verb, and `release` is `discard`. The engine, the git-config identity, and the append-only log are unchanged — only the tool's shape and the word moved. 

**D115.** **A conflicted join is a graded resolution ladder ending in a review-gated AI rung, never a dead end or a hand-off.** `tugarc-core`'s `join` builds the integration commit **off to the side** (`merge-tree --write-tree` stages → resolved blobs → `commit-tree` on the base → staleness-guarded fast-forward), so no checkout is ever half-merged and abort is free. Conflicts escalate: replay probe (round-by-round in-memory replay; a clean replay lands as linear rounds, shape change accepted) → `rerere` (enabled by default; replays recorded resolutions, and the ladder records its own driver/AI resolutions) → per-file `merge-file` → a PATH-discovered structured-merge driver (mergiraf by default, never bundled — AGPL) → the AI rung. The AI rung ([P32]) is a headless per-file scribe merge (base/ours/theirs + the arc's maintained draft as intent) on the existing `ScribeSpawner` seam, deterministically validated marker-free, streamed to the card as a `/btw`-style overlay — **never a Session card, never auto-committed**. The card reviews the pre-built candidate (resolved files badged by rung) and lands it on explicit confirm; non-content conflicts (delete/modify, binary, mode) short-circuit to honest "unresolved". [D114]

**D116.** **Every change lands through one lifecycle — Preflight → Draft → Land — and landing is the user's act, from the prompt entry.** The consolidation over [D112]/[D113]: buckets and rounds gather truth; an editable, durable, **machine-global** Draft (message + selection dispositions + edited-provenance in `changes.changeset_drafts` — the same scope axiom as `file_events`) proposes the landing; and the prompt-entry verbs **`/commit`** (main lane, two-beat: draft on the first beat, land on the second) and **`/join`** (arc lane, preview → squash with the join draft as message) execute it. The Changes shade — a modal `TugSheet` presentation over the transcript region with the prompt entry live beneath it — is where drafts are reviewed and edited (`TugMessageEditor` on the `TugTextEditor` substrate; the composer IS the display); an `edited` draft is never machine-clobbered (the shade's confirmed Regenerate is the only overwrite path). Landings are idle-gated at dispatch AND at the affordance; drafting stays live mid-turn. Skills draft, humans land: the `draft` skill (né `commit`) authors via `tugtool draft set` and never commits; `implement` ends by writing the arc's join draft and pointing at `/join <name>`; Claude's built-in `/commit` is shadowed dead by the local verb. Squash is the only join strategy in the UI (merge/rebase stay CLI-expert paths); release is shade-only with a discard preflight, and both join and release clear the arc's draft row so a reused arc name never inherits a dead arc's message. Every landing leaves a non-context receipt in the transcript; History badges joins by the `Tug-Arc:` trailer. The gathering verb is `tugtool preflight` (né `context` — "context" is AI-reserved vocabulary; a hidden alias holds for one release). Full doctrine: [tracking-changes.md](tracking-changes.md) § The landing workflow. 

**D117.** **The commit route is a `!changes` chip in the composer, orthogonal to the changes glance — the composer IS the message editor.** The realization of [D116]'s "the composer IS the display", refined to a prefix-chip model. There is **one bottom-anchored passive changes sheet** (`TugSheetContent presentation="shade" shadeAnchor="bottom" shadeAutoSize shadePassive`) that rises from the top of Z2 over the transcript (scrim + transcript-`inert`, no focus trap — focus stays in the composer below, [P17]); it is the read-only glance, driven purely by the `ShadeViewController`'s `changes` view. **Two orthogonal axes:** (1) the sheet's *visibility* (card chrome), and (2) the *commit route* — "the composer leads with a `!changes` command chip." The chip IS the route: while present, the prompt entry is the commit-message editor (the payload after the chip is the message), the slash/bang/mention machinery stands down, Z5 swaps to a cancel / auto-message / commit icon rail (the pencil-sparkles Auto-Message guarded by the Replace-message `TugConfirmPopover`), and submit lands the commit (`extractCommitMessage` strips the chip; `matchBangCommandLine` recognizes the same line) instead of sending to Claude. **⇧⌘C** (and `/commit`, `!changes`, Session ▸ Commit…) is the entry: on an empty composer it shows the sheet **and** enters the route (inserts the chip); on a non-empty composer it shows the sheet as a **glance only**, leaving the in-progress prompt untouched; toggled again it hides the sheet and, if the route was active, drops the chip. The session card owns the coupling (`commit-route-controller.ts` no longer knows about the shade); History and the route are mutually exclusive. The intermediate transcript-resident `TugCommitDialog` (an inline dialog with its own embedded editor + button rail) is **retired** — it duplicated the composer, blocked nothing, reserved transcript space, and left `!changes` inert; this model deletes all four problems and makes `!changes` a real routing. The Commit button's turn/pending/empty-changeset gate is JS `disabled`; the empty-message half is CSS-gated on the entry's `data-commit-empty` (the message with the chip stripped — `data-empty` can't be used, the chip makes the doc non-empty) so no per-keystroke React state is introduced ([L22]). **Uncommitted edits are durable:** every genuine user edit debounce-persists via `persistMessage` (backend maintained-draft engine → `entry.draft.message`, the source of truth), so edits survive card deactivation / reload; a cancel/re-enter resumes them. `tug-sheet.tsx` (`shadeAnchor` / `shadePassive`), `commit-route-controller.ts`, `tug-prompt-entry.tsx` (`commitRoute`, `buildCommitRouteState` / `extractCommitMessage`), `session-card.tsx` (route↔sheet coupling). **Superseded (chip + routing spelling only):** the `!changes` prefix chip and the routing framing are retired by [D119] — committing is the composer's one secondary resting mode, the editor holds the message alone, and `!changes` only teaches. The two-axis doctrine (sheet visibility vs. the active commit state), the composer-as-editor, the Z5 rail, the land gate, and the durable-draft persistence all stand and carry forward. [D116], [P17], [L22]

**Auto-Message feedback ([P06]).** Generating a commit message is a legible, cancellable, non-leaking act — the scribe is a separate headless `claude -p` child, wholly isolated from the interactive session's turn. While it streams: the composer goes read-only (`disabled` → `editable(false)`, so manual typing can't interfere), an **untinted wave caret** (the AI-thinking `TugProgressWave` glyph in the prose text color, a CM6 `Decoration.widget` rebuilt at `doc.length` every transaction — `wave-caret.ts`) rides the stream's tail in place of the suppressed native caret while the view scroll-follows it, and the **Auto-Message button stays lit** for the whole composition (a real filled-accent button via `emphasis`, pointer-neutralized on `data-drafting`). On settle the view scrolls back to the top so the message reads from its start. **Auto-Message is repeatable** — an empty field always FORCES regeneration (`requestDraft(true)`), so the [P03] edited-gate never swallows a repeat after the first edit — and the whole generation is **one undo step**: deltas stream with `addToHistory: false` (`restoreState`'s new opt) and the settle reverts-then-applies so ⌘Z removes the generated message and ⌘⇧Z restores it. **Cancel** is the X immediately left of the button, Escape, or Cmd-. — all three abort only the scribe child and **never reach the session-turn interrupt**: the leak is closed at two points because the window-level keybinding claims `CANCEL_DIALOG` before the entry-root listener when a turn is in flight, so the `CANCEL_DIALOG` responder cancels the draft first (turn-running case) and the entry-root capture listener handles Escape/Cmd-. directly (no-turn case). Cancel reverts the composer to its persisted pre-draft message (no half-stream kept). The abort is a `changeset_draft_cancel` CONTROL verb → a JoinHandle registry keyed by entry identity (`draft_engine.rs`); aborting drops the `kill_on_drop` child. The scribe prompt (`scribe.rs draft_ask`) forbids hard-wrapping — no column width, one unbroken line per subject/bullet. `wave-caret.ts` (`setWaveCaretActive`), `changeset-draft-store.ts` (`cancelDraft` + the `cancelled` → idle fold), `commit-route-controller.ts` (`cancelDraft`), `draft_engine.rs` (`DraftTaskRegistry` / `cancel_draft`), `agent_supervisor.rs` (`changeset_draft_cancel`). [P06], [L06], [L22]

**D118.** **One row grammar for changed files: `TugChangesList` rows are compact mono `TugListRow`s, every row expands into its diff, and the `/commit` receipt's file list IS a changes list — sha-backed.** The live glance, commit mode, and the durable receipt render the identical row (status letter leading; path + badges content; the monochrome `+N −M` pair, pop-out, and fold cue trailing) — the only differences are the diff source and which affordances are live. Three moves make this hold. (1) **The row layer moved off `BlockChrome` onto `TugListRow`** — a changed file has no lifecycle, so the tool-block header's lifecycle dot (and its header padding) had no business on a file row; the whole row is the fold's click target, the diff body mounts under it on expand ([L26] unmount-collapse), and the path link / trailing controls own their gestures. `TugListRow`/`TugListView` grew a **`density="compact"`** knob (context-published `rowDensity`; hairline block padding via the existing `--tugx-list-row-padding-*` tokens) and the changes list rides it by default — hundreds-of-files commits stay scannable. (2) **Every row is expandable because every file now diffs:** the head-flavor backend synthesizes a new-file unified diff per untracked path (`git diff --no-index -- /dev/null <path>` appended to `git diff HEAD` — `fetch_git_diff_with_untracked`), which also feeds the Auto-Message scribe (created-but-never-committed content reaches the prompt and the fingerprint; the old `(size, mtime)` side-band fold-in is retired). The old behavior — untracked rows silently rendering a disabled chevron — was status-dependent mystery, not design. (3) **The receipt composes `CommitChangesList`** over a third `DiffDescriptor` flavor, **`{kind:"commit", root, sha, paths}`** (`git diff-tree --no-commit-id --root -M -p <sha>` server-side — first-parent diff, root commit against the empty tree), replacing the static `FrozenChangesList`: counts render instantly from the frozen record, each row's hunks fetch lazily on first expand (a thousand-file commit costs nothing until you look), the pop-out opens a `Commit <sha>` Diff card, and a vanished sha (rebase, gc) degrades to an in-body notice while the rows stay intact. `root` is the exchange row's ledger-persisted `cwd`, so live and restored receipts resolve the same workspace with no side-band data. Diff counts everywhere are the shared `DiffSummaryBadges` atom — ghost `inherit` badges, **no green/red tint** (the monochrome doctrine [P27]; chromostereopsis made per-theme tinted counts unsolvable) — while the status mark keeps its tone. The status mark itself is a **colored single letter, not a glyph** (`statusMark`): green **N** (new — untracked or added), yellow **M** (modified or moved — renamed folds in), red **D** (deleted); a letter reads more legibly than a lucide file-icon at row size, and three letters cover every state the list surfaces. **The mark is one shared element, `TugStatusMark`, and the mapping is one function, `commit-format.ts`'s `statusMark`** — the commit hover's roster once painted its own `A`/`M`/`D`/`R` in its own four tones beside a file row spelling the identical commit in three, so the hover now renders this component and `DiffSummaryBadges` rather than a second drawing of them; `statusMark` takes any vocabulary that arrives (status words, porcelain codes, a bare letter) and answers in the house three. `tug-changes-list.tsx` (`ChangesFileRow` / `CommitChangesList`), `tug-status-mark.tsx`, `entity-tips.tsx`, `tug-list-row.tsx` (`density`), `diff-summary-badges.tsx`, `git-diff-store.ts` (`commit` flavor), `feeds/git.rs` (`fetch_git_diff_with_untracked` / `build_commit_diff_snapshot`), `session-commit-receipt-block.tsx`. [D117], [P01], [P08], [P27], [L06], [L26]
**D119.** **Commit is a composer mode, not a bang routing — the mode lives in the chrome, never in the document.** [D117]'s prefix-chip model made committing a cousin of `!shell`/`!btw`/`!find`/`!history`, and every seam showed: the chip did double duty (an inert pre-submit routing token AND in-mode dressing — you could hold a `!changes` chip with normal Z5 and not be in the mode), Z4A hid the routing picker behind a dead icon, and Z4B kept the Claude-session chips (CLAUDE CODE / MODE / MODEL / EFFORT) that describe *sending a prompt* while you were doing something else entirely. The repair separates three ideas that had been conflated: **bang commands** are per-submission routings (four of them — shell, btw, find, history); **the Changes glance** is a view (the read-only bottom sheet); **commit is the prompt entry's one secondary resting mode** (`commit-mode-controller.ts`). In the mode the editor holds **only the message** — no chip, no `extractCommitMessage`, submit lands the document verbatim — which deletes the ambiguous half-state outright: there is no way to wear commit dressing without being in the mode, because the dressing is chrome. **Z4A** keeps the `!` routing picker in its slot but **disabled** — routings are meaningless while composing a commit message, and the Z5 ✕ is the mode's single way out, so Z4A carries no exit of its own (an interactive Commit chip was tried first and cut: a second exit was redundant, and the slot must not shift). **Z4B swaps** (the Table T01 move shell/find already made): the Claude chips unmount; the commit cluster is **Project** (where it lands) + **Changes** (`N files`, click toggles the changes sheet without leaving the mode — the first in-mode sheet control). Z5's cancel / auto-message / commit rail stands unchanged. **Entry points unify on `enter(seed)`**: ⇧⌘C on an empty composer, `/commit [message]`, Session ▸ Commit…; ⌃⌘C is a muscle-memory alias for the ⇧⌘C toggle (it no longer seeds a chip). **`!changes` is retired to a plain unknown bang**: the registry drops to four and the `!` picker lists four; a submitted `!changes` is no longer special-cased — it falls to the shell escape hatch exactly like any unregistered `!foobar` (this app has a single user who will never meet a teaching notice for a feature they never shipped). Deliberately NOT built: a generalized composer-modes framework — prompt and commit are the only resting modes ([P01] stands; everything else is per-submission), and one honest special case beats a framework with two tenants. `commit-mode-controller.ts`, `tug-prompt-entry.tsx` (`commitMode`, `buildCommitModeState`, the disabled-in-mode Z4A picker), `session-card.tsx` (commit cluster, `TOGGLE_CHANGES_VIEW`), `bang-commands.ts` (registry drops `changes`), `keybinding-map.ts` (⌃⌘C alias). [D117], [P01], [L22], [L26]

---

**D120.** **A session can *claim* a file — an explicit, intentional attribution act, decoupled from editing.** Changeset attribution has two tiers, and only one claims a file: a **proof** row (`origin: exact`/`replay`) means a tool call named the exact file (Write/Edit/MultiEdit/NotebookEdit); a **correlation** row (`origin: bash`/`turn`) is a whole-tree fingerprint delta around a Bash command, recorded as a "likely" *hint* on the unattributed bucket, never proof (a tree-delta can't tell the session's own write from the user's hand-save during the command, or from another session on the same checkout — `changes.rs::compute_changes`). The gap this exposed: **claiming was welded to editing** — the only way for a session to own a file it touched via `perl`/`sed` (bracket-only) or by hand was to *re-edit it with a proof tool*. `origin: "claim"` fixes that: a third proof origin (`origin_is_proof` on both the writer `attribution.rs` and reader `tugchanges-core/ledger.rs`) that promotes a hinted file straight into the session's entry, written by an explicit claim rather than a side effect of editing. **tugcast owns the write** (tugchanges-core opens the ledger read-only; the aggregate recomputes only on tugcast's process-global bump): the `changeset_claim` CONTROL verb in `agent_supervisor.rs` records one `claim` file-event per repo-relative path via `record_file_event`, then fires `changeset_all_bump`. **Two surfaces, one handler.** The deck sends a `changeset_claim` WS control frame (a **Claim** affordance on each unattributed "likely" row → `ChangesRouteController.claim` → `ChangesetVerbStore.claim`). The CLI (`tugtool claim <paths>`) POSTs `/api/tell {action:"changeset_claim", …}`; `tell_handler` bridges `changeset_*` actions into the same `handle_control` (the single source of truth shared with the WebSocket ingress) under a synthetic client id, since a tell has no connection — gated to the `changeset_` prefix so client-scoped verbs (`spawn_session`) stay off the HTTP surface. Idempotent (re-claiming writes another proof row, composed identically); a self-claim never marks contention. This is the intentional, first-class counterpart to editing — never "write the file to claim it". `feeds/attribution.rs` + `tugchanges-core/ledger.rs` (`origin_is_proof`), `agent_supervisor.rs` (`do_changeset_claim`), `server.rs` (`tell_handler` bridge), `tugtool` (`claim` verb), `changeset-verb-store.ts` / `changes-route-controller.ts` / `tug-changes-list.tsx` (`ChangesFileRow` Claim). [D118], [P03]

**Orphan surfacing — the open-card path is fresh-on-look.** The aggregate composes only projects **open in the `WorkspaceRegistry`** and recomposes only on the process-global bump (the per-workspace FS watcher, ledger writes) — so a working-tree change that lands while no FS event fires can leave the cached snapshot stale ("No changes" while the tree is dirty). Hardening: a side-effect-free **`changeset_refresh`** CONTROL verb fires the same bump (no payload, no reply; emission is diff-suppressed, so a client sees a frame only when the tree actually drifted), and the Session card fires it on **Changes-shade open** (`ChangesRouteController.refresh` → `ChangesetVerbStore.refresh`, wired at the shade-open effect in `session-card.tsx`) — so opening Changes always reflects live working-tree truth. Orphans in a project with **no** open card stay invisible to the UI by design (unwatched, uncomposed); `tugtool claim <paths> --project <dir>` reclaims those headlessly.

**Orphaned bucket — a file stranded on a dead session.** Diagnosis (via the observability endpoint below) corrected the earlier "zero-row/trashed" theory: closing a session does **not** delete its rows (only *trashing* cascades a delete). So a file its author proof-owned stays *owned* after the session closes (`live: false`) — never entering the unattributed bucket — yet no live card surfaces another session's changeset entry, so it falls into a gap and reads as "No changes." Fix: `compose_snapshot` **lifts** a file whose every proof-owner is non-live into a dedicated `orphaned` bucket (`OrphanedFile` carries the dead `prior_owner_name`/`id`); a file any *live* session co-owns stays in that live entry, so a file orphans only when all its owners are dead. The Changes shade renders `orphaned` as a claimable section ("orphaned — claim to bring into this session") with the same per-row **Claim** affordance the unattributed bucket uses. Semantics ([D120] extension, user-locked): closing keeps rows, so re-opening the originating session **auto-re-owns** its untouched orphans (plain composition — a live owner's proof-dirty files show in its entry; a committed/reverted file is spent by the row-liveness rule and won't silently re-attach). But **claim severs the prior owner** — `do_changeset_claim` calls `SessionLedger::sever_file_ownership_except(project_dir, paths, claimant)` to delete other sessions' rows for the claimed paths, so a claimed file is "no longer an orphan, nor owned by the originator." `changeset.rs` (lift + unattributed exclusion), `tugcast-core/types.rs` (`OrphanedFile`, `ChangesetSnapshot.orphaned`), `session_ledger.rs` (`sever_file_ownership_except`), `agent_supervisor.rs` (`do_changeset_claim` sever), `changeset-types.ts` / `changes-route-controller.ts` / `tug-changes-list.tsx` / `session-changes-view.tsx` (orphaned entry kind + Claim).

**Changeset observability.** `GET /api/changesets` (loopback, read-only; `server.rs` → `AgentSupervisor::compose_changeset_aggregate`) returns the live aggregate composed fresh over the registry + ledger — ground truth for "what compose produces right now," independent of emission/diff-suppression. `tugtool host changesets` (`--json` for the full snapshot) prints per-project `changesets`/`unattributed`/`orphaned` counts with each owner's liveness — the tool that pinned the dead-owner gap. `compose_aggregate` also traces per-project counts at debug level. NB: a throwaway `tugcast --source-tree` shares the machine-global `changes.db`, so a live claim through it mutates the real ledger — verify the claim/sever path at the unit layer, not by driving the shared ledger.

**D122.** **The composer has exactly two routes — Prompt and Changes — and the `!` layer is retired with no vestige.** A **route** is defined as *a mode that owns the composer's whole document*. Exactly two things do: typing a prompt, and authoring a commit message. Everything else is either a **one-shot verb** (a slash command — it consumes one submission and leaves you where you were) or lives **outside the composer entirely** (find). The four things previously called "bang routings" — `!shell`, `!btw`, `!find`, `!history` — were verbs wearing a routing's sigil: none ever swapped the document, and calling them routes was the original error. This decision replaces [D110]'s three-recipient group and [D119]'s "commit is a mode, not a bang routing" framing (which was right about commit and wrong only in the company it kept).

**The Z4A group is a view, not a second home for the selection.** `CommitModeController` already swapped the document on enter/exit (stashing the prompt draft in `preCommitDraftRef`, restoring it verbatim, persisting the message through the changeset draft store), so the two-item `TugChoiceGroup` renders `commitActive ? "changes" : "prompt"` and dispatches `SELECT_VALUE` into `enter()` / `exitCommitMode()`. It holds no state, which is what makes every existing entry and exit path — ⇧⌘C, `/commit`, Session ▸ Show Changes, a successful land, Cancel ✕, Escape, the shade's self-close — move the visible tab with zero new code. It is deliberately **not disabled in commit mode** ([D119] disabled the `!` button there): the group is how you leave Changes. **No controller means no group** — `TugPromptEntry`'s `commitMode` prop is optional and the Component Gallery mounts the entry without one, so `Z4A` renders empty there rather than a Changes segment that cannot act. Consequence worth writing down: this quietly makes `CommitModeController` the route selector, which is right for two routes and wrong for three — a third route means lifting the selection into a small route controller with commit mode as one of its values.

**The bang layer retires with no vestige.** `lib/bang-commands.ts` is deleted along with every consumer: the `!` button and its `TugPopupMenu`, the `bangCommandCompletionProvider` and its `"!"` trigger, the ⌃⌘S/B/G/H chip-seed chords and the ⌃⌘C `!changes` alias, `bangCommandSurfaces`, the submit-path `matchBangCommandLine` call, the `isBangCommand` sigil branches in `command-atom.ts` and `slash-commands.ts`, and the `SHOW_SLASH_COMMAND_NOTICE` branch that taught `/shell` → `!shell` (a redirect that pointed the wrong way by the end). Two namespaces with two sigils was a tax paid on every surface — picker, completion popup, chip label, reconstructed command line, help text, unknown-command notice each carried a bang branch. **A leading `!` is ordinary prose again**: `!ls` goes to Claude as text. That is the intended behavior, not a regression — the shell auto-router (`shell-line-classifier`) carries a bare `ls` to the shell, which is a better path than the bang ever was. No notice, no redirect, no deprecation shim: a `!`-specific notice would itself be the vestige.

**`/shell` and `/btw` demote to arg-taking local slash commands**, their surfaces moved verbatim. `/shell <cmd>` is the deliberate override under the auto-router — the classifier decides by default, and a user who knows better can force it, which costs one registry entry and removes the "what if it misroutes" objection to deleting `!shell`. `/btw`'s duplication resolves itself: with the `!` menu gone there is one *place* BTW lives and `/btw` is merely how you *ask* — one feature with two organs, not two doors ([D108] stands unchanged). That place is now the **placard**: the Z2 diet dropped the BTW cell, and the answer surface pops from the top of the Z2 strip, right-aligned to the host card, rather than from a cell of its own. `SideQuestionStore` and the placard body are unchanged — only the anchor moved, and the cell it was anchored to was the widest thing on the row that nothing else needed. **Neither route has a typeable name.** A slash command is a one-shot verb that returns you where you were; a route is a mode you stay in, and the two do not belong in one namespace. `/prompt` could not have worked at all — the Changes route owns the document, so `performSubmit` lands the message verbatim and returns before slash interception, which left it reachable only from the route it already names — and the plan's `/changes`, shipped without a twin, was a half-namespace whose behavior nobody could predict from the other half. Both are gone: the route gestures are the Z4A tabs, ⇧⌘P / ⇧⌘C, and Escape, and `/commit [message]` remains the *verb* (it enters with a seed and is an act of committing, not an act of looking). Every slash command therefore leaves the route alone, which is what keeps "`/` is a typing accelerator" true without making the `/` key itself a route gesture. **`!history` is deleted as a route only** — the History shade, ⇧⌘H, Session ▸ Show History, and the `/tugplug:history` skill are untouched; the route was the unused part, and `/tugplug:history <question>` is a real Claude command that replaces the `!history <question>` variant directly.

**Find lifts out of the composer into a ⌘F bar** (`TugFindBar`, extracted from `TextCardFindBar` — one find face across the deck, over a host-supplied `FindSession`). Routing find through the prompt editor's submit path was a category error: it made find a *submission*, which is why it needed a dissolve-on-next-submit rule and an Escape-when-empty rule living inside the composer. All three vestiges of that are deleted — the submit-path dissolve, the empty-editor Escape branch, and the `findActive`-gated `TugFindCluster` in `Z4B`. ⌘F needed no Swift work: the chord was already plumbed end to end (Edit ▸ Find… → `sendControl("find")` → `action-dispatch` → the chain), and `host-menu-state` derives the menu item's enablement from `chain.validateAction(FIND)` — so the *only* missing piece was one responder registration on the session card, and registering it is simultaneously what un-greys the menu item. **Closing the bar ends the search** (`findSession.clear()`), but the **query survives** to the next ⌘F, pre-filled and selected — standard macOS behavior, and what makes clear-on-close painless. There is deliberately **no `/find`**: a `/find <query>` command would reintroduce the composer-as-search-box shape through a different sigil, and ⌘F is more discoverable than any slash command. See [D97] for the bar's geometry and its non-shade status.

**⌘F toggles, with no carve-out.** The chord that summons a mode is the chord that dismisses it — the shape ⇧⌘C already had on Changes. It must fire *from inside the bar too*, and that is the load-bearing half rather than an edge case: ⌘F lands the caret in the query field, so the second press of any real toggle attempt comes from inside. A bar that answered `FIND` with its own responder — re-summoning the field instead of closing — would make the toggle unreachable by the only gesture anyone makes. The bar therefore registers no `FIND` handler at all; the host's is the only one.

**Find and Changes are one slot.** Both are modes that take over the bottom of the card, and neither is a shade the other can rise over: opening the bar leaves Changes (through `CommitModeController.leave()`, which persists a typed message the way Cancel and the Z4A tab do), and entering Changes dismisses the bar. The exclusion is wired **off the mode's active flag**, not per door, which is what makes every entrance agree — ⇧⌘C, `/commit`, the tab, the Session menu, the failed-land re-entry.

**The bar joins the card's focus cycle; it does not bring a walk of its own.** Its four controls — query, options, Previous, Next — are stops in `SESSION_CYCLE_GROUP` at orders **8…11**, between the Z4 toolbar (0…7) and the Z2 status cells, which is exactly where the bar sits on screen (the cycle reads the card upward from its bottom edge). They register through the *same* `CycleScope` the prompt entry uses — a second `CycleScope` around the bar, which is what that primitive was always for — and they take a row in `cycleSpatialOrder`, so the arrow plane covers them like every other row. The stops exist only while the bar does; the walk skips what is not rendered, and the spatial row drops out empty.

This is worth stating as a rule, because the first attempt got it wrong: **a new surface on a card joins the card's authored order — it never declares a parallel one.** A private group in the base mode "worked" in the sense that Tab moved within it, and was wrong in every way that matters: two orders on one card, no spatial order at all on the new one, and ⌥⇥ walking a set of stops the user could see but not reach.

**Tab splits by the KIND of surface, not by whether it is empty.** This entry used to read "an empty text field spends Tab on movement", with the field handing the gesture to its host to enter the cycle at its own seat. KBF mode ([focus-language.md](focus-language.md), *The mode division comes first*) replaced that with a structural rule: a **multi-line** surface owns Tab because it indents; a **single-line `INPUT`** does not, so Tab there engages the mode and takes one step, the same as the nowhere state. Content never enters into it — which is the point, since the emptiness reading was one of the three ambient mechanisms the mode retired.

Return and ⇧Return follow the caret for free: the bar's find keys live in its substrate's `Prec.high` keymap, so they are the ↓ / ↑ buttons' twins in the query field and are submit again in the composer. Dismissing the bar returns the keyboard to the composer **only when the bar was holding it** (`TugFindBarHandle.holdsKeyboard`, which reads both registers — DOM focus for the caret, the projected `data-key-view` mark for an engine-routed ring), and it returns it *the way it got there*: cycling exits the cycle and lets `restingFocus` land the caret, because a raw editor claim under a pushed mode is the focus-language bug that leaves ring and caret disagreeing. Escape at a bar control belongs to the **cycle** (it ascends), which is the language working; the bar's own `cancelDialog` handler covers the pointer path, where the chain has promoted to the bar and nothing else would answer.

**The cluster sits at the trailing edge, count first.** Count, then Case / Word / Grep, then ↑ / ↓ — result, then the controls that shape it, in one group. Centring the cluster (the shell's `toolbarCenter`) put a card's width of empty toolbar between the count and the buttons that change it, and needed an absolute-centring override to look right at all; the trailing group needs no CSS of its own.

**Chords.** ⌘F toggles the find bar; ⌘G / ⇧⌘G are unchanged (they stay registered on the prompt entry so ⌘G cycles with the caret in the composer, gated on the session holding matches — true exactly while the bar is open). ⇧⌘C keeps toggling Changes; with two routes a toggle is also a complete round-trip, so no Prompt-specific Swift menu item is needed. **⇧⌘P selects Prompt** and `CYCLE_PERMISSION_MODE` moves to **⌃⌘P** in *both* doors — the `keybinding-map.ts` entry and the Swift Session ▸ Permission Mode ▸ Cycle key equivalent — because either alone leaves the menu bar swallowing the chord. ⌘/ keeps `OPEN_COMMAND_PICKER` but now means "open the `/` completion popup," implemented as a plain editor insert of a leading `/`; the old Radix `focus()`-then-synthetic-Enter workaround left with the popup it worked around. ⌃⌘S / ⌃⌘B / ⌃⌘C / ⌃⌘O / ⌃⌘H are deleted, which is what leaves the ⌃⌘ band free for the permission cycle rather than making it a graveyard.

Originating plan: Supersedes [D110] (three recipients) and amends [D119] (the "four bang routings" it contrasts commit against no longer exist). [D97], [D108], [D111], [L02], [L03], [L06], [L11], [L23], [L24], [L26]

**D123.** **A slot's stack gets a local switching surface — a title-bar badge, a picker built from the store, and ⌘R — and the stack itself is derived, never stored.** [D121] settled the geometry: any number of panes may hold one slot, assignment always raises, and the top one is what you see. What it left was a deck that hid its own evidence — `pane-occlusion-controller.ts` stamps a fully covered pane `data-occluded="true"` and CSS turns that into `visibility: hidden`, so a buried card contributed not one pixel to say it existed, and the Cards list, a remote surface, was the only way to reach it. D123 is the interaction decision that sits on D121's geometry. **The stack is derived from `DeckState.panes` and never stored** (`slotStackOf(state, slot)` beside `findLensPane`): membership and z-order are both already fully determined by state the deck owns, so a stored copy could only ever disagree with it — and deriving keeps the surface automatically correct across `assignCardToSlot`, drag/resize eviction, kind changes that clamp slots, and pane close. No field is added to `TugPaneState`, no serialization version moves, and the collapse case needs no notification: when a peer leaves and the depth drops to 1 the badge stops rendering, and the prop the picker already reads is the signal that closes it (a stale-open bit would otherwise unmount the trigger with the menu up, stranding keyboard focus on a removed node and re-mounting already-open). `DeckCanvas` resolves it — the same vantage point that resolves `placement`, for the same reason: a pane cannot work out chain-wide facts from its own state — and hands each pane a display-resolved `SlotStackEntry[]` rather than raw pane records, so `CardTitleBar` renders its menu from props alone and never reads a store ([L10]). **The badge shows on every pane in the stack, not only the front one.** It describes the *slot*, and panes in one slot may differ in width (the imposer never touches width), so a wider buried pane peeks out title bar and all — suppressing its badge would make the one title bar the user can see the one that stays silent. Occlusion already solves the common case: a fully covered pane is hidden, badge included, so a same-width stack shows exactly one. The render condition is therefore `slotStack.length > 1` and nothing else — no "am I the top?" test, which would need a second cross-pane fact. **The picker is built from store data, never from revealed DOM.** Showing the buried title bars as live DOM would mean bracketing the gesture with `paneOcclusionGesture.begin`/`.end`, un-hiding every pane on the deck for its duration and re-hiding them on a settle timer — a large, timing-sensitive cost for a list of strings the store already holds, and a pile of peeking title bars is a worse answer to "let me pick one of these" than a menu is. **A row is a miniature of the title bar it stands for**: it draws that pane's own `CardMeta.icon` ahead of that pane's own title-bar text, in the title bar's order, so the menu reads as a stack of small title bars rather than as a list of strings — both ride `SlotStackEntry`, resolved by `DeckCanvas` because the title bar renders its picker from props alone and reaches for neither a registry nor a store. **And a pane's name is produced in exactly one place, `lib/pane-title.ts`.** A pane's title is not a stored field: it composes the active card's registry title, the group name a multi-tab pane carries, and the live override a card publishes on `cardTitleStore` when its identity resolves at runtime. Two rules for that existed before this — the title bar's composition, and a simpler `CardState.title` fallback chain in the menu-state projection — and they disagreed exactly where it mattered most: a Session card bound to a project read `test-repo/petit-thaw` on its title bar and `Untitled` in every list that named it, because only the title bar consulted the override store. The module now holds the rule (`composePaneTitleBarText`, pure over the three parts, called by `CardTitleBar` which already holds them) and its resolver (`paneTitleBarTextFor`, which does the registry lookup and the override read for callers holding deck state), and all three naming surfaces adopt it — the title bar, the picker, and the Window menu's pane list, whose `Untitled` entries this also fixes. The override is not deck state, so each consumer subscribes to `cardTitleStore` in its own idiom: `DeckCanvas` through `useSyncExternalStore` on a new monotonic `version` (a per-card `get` cannot be a stable snapshot, and several panes' names are needed at once), and `initHostMenuState` by re-projecting from a plain subscription. That revision must be bumped *before* the listeners run, or a `useSyncExternalStore` subscriber reads the value it already had and nothing recomputes — a picker that notifies and does not repaint. **The badge stays lit while its menu is down**, in the pressed pose it already owns rather than a new colour, because a menu being open is a control being held — the macOS menu-bar reading, where the title that opened the menu keeps its highlight until it closes. Radix stamps `data-state="open"` on the trigger, so one CSS rule covers all three doors, including the two that never touch the badge. Its inline padding is derived from the square icon buttons beside it (`(height − icon-size) / 2`, their optical inset) rather than taking the stock `sm` 1rem, which set the one non-square control in the cluster 9px further from its neighbour than they sit from each other and split the row into two visual groups; the count is `tabular-nums` so a raise cannot jog the cluster, and nothing is reserved for a second digit — a ten-deep slot is rare enough that parking its width beside every badge would just restore the gap. **Three doors, one opener, one raise.** The badge is a ghost `TugButton` acting as a `TugPopupMenu` trigger, so the pointer path needs no open plumbing at all; the keyboard and Cmd-click paths drive the menu through a new optional controlled `open`/`onOpenChange` pair on `TugPopupMenu` (absent, every existing call site keeps its exact current uncontrolled behavior), because synthesizing a click on the trigger would be a guess at whichever DOM event Radix listens on and a lie about what the user did. All three converge on one `CardTitleBarHandle.revealStack`, reached the way `CLOSE_PANE` already reaches `requestClose`. Choosing a row raises through `transferFocusForActivation` + `activateCard` — what `assignCardToSlot` does, and for its stated reason: a bare `activateCard` flips the first responder but skips the focus transfer, so the outgoing card never saves its focus bag and the incoming one gets no caret until clicked. **Cmd-click resolves in the drag machine's own no-travel branch, so Cmd-drag survives.** Cmd-drag on a title bar is meaningful twice over — the Mac convention of moving a background window without raising it, and the gesture that *evicts* an imposed pane from its slot — so opening a menu on Cmd-*press* would kill both. `handleDragStart` records `event.metaKey` beside the origin it already snapshots, and `onPointerUp`'s existing `if (!dragMoved.current)` branch (already commented "the pointer never travelled, so this was a click on the title bar") acts on it; a drag past `DRAG_MOVE_THRESHOLD_PX` latches `dragMoved` and never arrives. No second gesture handler exists to fight the frame's pointer capture — one could not see the release anyway, since `handleDragStart` captures on the *frame* and every pointer event retargets there — and deferring `onDragStart` until travel would run `captureFocusForDragStart` after the mousedown blur it exists to beat. `gesture-interpreter.ts` is untouched: its `metaKey` branch already suppresses activation, which is exactly the posture wanted, and the title bar already carries `data-tug-fr-preserve`. **The chord is menu-owned and depth-gated.** ⌘R is `Go ▸ Reveal Stack`, not a `keybinding-map.ts` entry, because AppKit resolves a menu key equivalent before the `WKWebView` sees the keydown; freeing it moved `Maker ▸ Reload` to ⇧⌘R.
