## Pane Arrival & Column Height

**D194.** **A new card in a split column always appears at the bottom.** Every path that seats a pane in a slot — `addCard`, the Layout card's slot assignment, a title-bar drop with no zone index — names the arrival at the END of the column's stored order in the same commit that gives it the slot, through one pure rule, `withMemberSeated`. Before this the arrival was left unnamed and took the fallback reading, sorted by pane id among every other unnamed member: the first card opened into a split landed under its sitter, and the second landed above or below the first on the toss of two uuids, so nobody could say where a new card would appear. The fallback still exists, for members a legacy record never named, and it never decides a newcomer. A drop whose zone advertised an index is the one seat that is not the bottom, because honoring it is the indicator's promise; a stacked column's order is z and is not written here; a pane re-assigned to the slot it already stands in has not arrived and keeps its place. `tugdeck/src/lib/layout-imposer.ts` (`withMemberSeated`), `tugdeck/src/deck-manager.ts` (`_impositionSeating`). [P07], [pane-model.md](pane-model.md)

**D195.** *(Amended by [pane-model.md](pane-model.md)'s hidden arrival — the bid is no longer measured off-screen ahead of `addCard`'s commit: the card is committed hidden at the seat it will take, and the bid is written at the reveal from the sheet's own report while it stood there. The one-commit rule the bid exists for is unchanged; which commit it is written in is not. Kept for what it settled.)* *(Further amended by the `arrival-even-division` arc — a sitter's claim is the division the hand made rather than the height it happens to be drawing at. Only a member the column's `shares` record names brings a claim; a member nobody has weighed claims nothing and divides the unclaimed run equally with the newcomer, so an arrival into a column nobody has sashed comes out even and the arrival agrees with the mode flip, which was already even. A division the hand made is untouched, and the folded sitter's tier is untouched with it — a folded member is pinned by its ceiling rather than by a claim, so narrowing the claim leaves it exactly where it was.)* **A column member's height is one rule, and the exact-height pin is retired.** Four mechanisms decided a member's height and three of them meant the same thing: the stack's registered floor, the floor a pane-modal sheet reserves while it is up, the height a card declares while it is standing unbound, and the folded card's band. The first three are **floors** and `placeMembers` takes the largest of them; the fourth is the only genuine ceiling the deck has ([D185]). What forced the decision is what the third one was written as: an **exact height** — floor and ceiling with a weight of zero, the folded form's mechanism borrowed for something that was never a band — so a new Session card opening into a split column with hundreds of pixels to spare stood at exactly the 618px it declared, the column read as a wall of ceilings that could not fill its run, and the surplus was left beneath the card as a dead band. **The pin becomes an opening bid.** It is a floor for the arrival window, written inside `addCard`'s own commit so nothing re-targets a settle already in flight ([D194]'s one-commit rule), superseded by the first measurement at least as high as it, and cleared when the sheet goes — no card-state transition is party to it, so the drop at the binding commit is gone, and a claim BELOW the standing bid supersedes nothing, because a member must not shrink under a sheet that is standing on it. **The unbound form is a policy rather than a scalar**: `CardRegistration.unboundSizePolicy`, read by `getStackSizePolicy(ids, { unbound })` beside the folded one, declaring a `min.height` and no `max.height` — which is exactly how it differs from the folded policy it used to borrow. **The chrome arithmetic between a panel and the member under it lives in the deck, once**: `memberFloorForSheetPanel` adds the pane's title bar and the 1px its sheet clip drops below it, plus the gap the clamp keeps against the canvas, less the gap the imposition already leaves under a column's last member. The sheet reports the panel it measured and the deck does the rest, which is what made the measured reservation and the declared height denominate one quantity instead of two that sat 64px apart — the reservation used to be inert on the very card it was for. **And a newcomer arrives weighted**: `arrivalSharesOf` divides the run the sitters do not claim among the arrival and every sitter that claims nothing — a sitter the record NAMES claims what it stands at, capped by its own ceiling, and a member nobody has weighed claims nothing — and makes a claiming sitter yield only what a floor that division cannot cover needs, storing the answer through the division's own inverse so the next re-division agrees with what the eye saw. An unnamed newcomer weighed 1 against whatever the hand had sashed its neighbours to, which is a share of the run nobody chose. Proven on the live app over the arrangement that reported it: a folded sitter keeps its tier, the arrival takes every pixel beneath it, the column's last member reaches the bottom of the run, and the picker fits whole inside the card that took the room — with the retired ceiling reading patched back in to show both claims fail without the change. `tugdeck/src/deck-store-selectors.ts` (`placeMembers`), `tugdeck/src/lib/layout-imposer.ts` (`arrivalSharesOf`, `allocatePlaceHeights`), `tugdeck/src/lib/sheet-reservation.ts` (`memberFloorForSheetPanel`), `tugdeck/src/lib/opening-bid.ts`, `tugdeck/src/card-registry.ts` (`unboundSizePolicy`, `getUnboundSizePolicy`), `tugdeck/src/deck-manager.ts` (`sheetClaimWith`, `_arrivalShares`), `tugdeck/src/components/tugways/cards/session-card-registration.tsx`; `tests/app-test/at0569-sheet-reservation.test.ts`, `tests/app-test/at0571-picker-card-arrival.test.ts`. [D185], [D194], [pane-model.md](pane-model.md)

**D196.** **One rule chooses every fresh slotted card's slot, and the caller names only where the gesture came from.** Under a multi-slot arrangement `addCard` takes an `origin` — the card the gesture was made in — and never a slot; the deck ranks the arrangement from that card through one pure rule, `chooseOpeningSlot`. The keys, in order: **nearest** first (distance in slots), then an **empty** slot before a stacked one, then **right** before left. The origin's own slot is never the answer, so an open never covers the card that asked for it. **A split column is not a landing place**: it qualifies only when no empty or stacked slot does, and then the nearest split takes the arrival at its bottom ([D194]). A **wall** — a split column whose upper members are folded — ranks after every ordinary split, however near, and when one does take the arrival the commit folds the column around it, so a wall stays a wall. The origin contract is how a menu or a link reaches the rule: a right-click does not move first responder, so every opener — the annotation menu, a primary click on a file or commit reference, the commit identity menu, a changes or history row, a receipt, a slash command — carries `originCardId`, read from the card the gesture was made in (`hostCardIdOf`, `useCardId`), and the deck falls back to the first responder only when the gesture names no card, and to the arrangement's own anchor when neither holds a slot. That anchor is the middle slot in fit and the middle slot the band shows in flow, and in flow a deck-anchored open ranks the wholly visible slots first, then the clipped ones, then the whole arrangement. Before this there were two rules — a left-neighbour helper for some openers, a center-slot one for the rest — and a split column was a landing place like any other, so where a card opened depended on which verb opened it. `tugdeck/src/lib/opening-placement.ts` (`readOpeningDeck`, `chooseOpeningSlot`), `tugdeck/src/deck-manager.ts` (`openingSlotFor`, `addCard`), `tugdeck/src/lib/layout-imposer.ts` (`flowVisibleSlots`), `tugdeck/src/lib/annotator/annotation-element.ts` (`hostCardIdOf`); `tests/app-test/at0369-open-file-nearest-slot.test.ts`, `tests/app-test/at0599-menu-open-beside-host.test.ts`. [D194], [pane-model.md](pane-model.md)

**D198.** **The session sparkline's picture is a pure function of the activity store's bins and the clock, drawn fresh on every tick onto a viewport-sized canvas the render worker owns.** The tape it replaces was two pictures kept in agreement: a canvas painted at an epoch origin and a WAAPI transform scrolling that canvas under a viewport, plus the machinery the agreement needed — a committed origin, a rebase proposal, an acknowledgement, a watchdog, a registration self-check, a dormancy machine and an `IntersectionObserver` gate. Every reported defect was a disagreement between those two pictures, and each round of hardening ([D133]) closed one seam and left the rest: the tape still jumped spuriously and still stuck without advancing. **The answer is not a better protocol but the removal of the thing a protocol was for.** There is now ONE picture. On every store event the surface posts `{headBin, bins, hold}` — the whole retained window, and nothing about time — and the instrument computes the picture for the moment it is drawing from those bins and its own `Date.now()`: each bin edge lands at `x = width − (displayNow − binEdge) · pxPerSec`, with the rolling one-second sum, the response curve, the clamp and the held tail all arithmetic inside the draw. **Both threads read the same wall clock and the bin index is absolute**, so there is no clock to convert, no origin to agree about, and a message that arrives late, out of order or not at all costs one frame rather than a divergence — the next message is the whole truth again. `displayNow` is two bins behind the clock, which is what hides the open bin still filling. **The tick is a finisher and it stops itself**: a ~15Hz redraw whose only state is a timer handle, armed by a store event and stopped when every plotted value across the retained window is equal — including the drained tail, because a steady hum is flat at a nonzero value and a stop that read value-stability alone would freeze the tape at it ([D7]'s distinction between the data's future and the pixels' present, which this surface is the source of). At rest the instrument holds no timer, and the zero reading is the stylesheet's baseline bar rather than anything the canvas has to have painted. **The canvas is viewport-sized and worker-owned**, so no style is mutated and no main-thread rendering update is scheduled — the named [L13] carve-out ([animation-doctrine.md](animation-doctrine.md#instrument-carve-out)), scoped to worker-owned canvases so it cannot be cited for a main-thread style loop. Where `transferControlToOffscreen` is unavailable the identical class runs on the page against an ordinary 2D context: a platform without transferable canvases loses the thread, not the picture, and because it is one class rather than a parallel drawing path there is nothing for the two to disagree about. The curve crosses the thread boundary as a **spec** rather than a function, since a structured clone carries no closures; a caller that builds its own curve routes to the main-thread host. `GaugeMeter` gained real level-per-bin history on `RateMeter`'s grid — a gauge row had no past at all before, which is why a gauge tape could only ever be flat. *Do not re-propose*: an epoch origin with a transform scrolling over it (that IS the two-picture model, whatever the protocol around it); an acknowledgement or a rebase (nothing waits on anything now, so there is no window for a paint to land in the wrong order); a visibility gate finer than `document.visibilityState` (an `IntersectionObserver` keyed on a scroller the element may be reparented out of is how a live tape stops for good); or a dormancy machine (quiet is constructed by the tick stopping itself, never detected). `tugdeck/src/lib/sparkline-instrument.ts` (the arithmetic, the draw, the tick, the curve library and specs), `lib/sparkline-host.ts` (which thread, and nothing else), `lib/workers/sparkline-render-worker.ts` (the worker host and the `state` message), `lib/activity-meter.ts` (`GaugeMeter`'s binned history), `components/tugways/tug-sparkline.tsx` (the surface: canvas claim, colour resolution, store subscription, two attribute stamps). Supersedes [D133]; `lib/sparkline-tape.ts` and `drawSparkline`'s epoch geometry are deleted with it. [L06], [L13], [L26], [L27]

**D199.** **A release is four acts the window can carry — land, push, edit the notes, ship — and the product learns what a release *is* from three strings the checkout supplies.** Shipping Tug took seven typed commands in a terminal, in an order only the person who wrote them remembered, so the whole of it is now surfaces: `/commit` writes, `/push` sends, the Changes shade's release-notes row becomes an editor in place, and `/release` opens a fourth shade where the check runs, the dispatch is gated, and the queued run is watched to an end. The skill `/release-tug` is the one typed thing left, and it is a state probe rather than a script — it reads where the release stands (`shipped`, `in-flight`, `bump-abandoned`) and says which act is next. **The product knows nothing about releasing.** `[tugtool.release]` carries `check`, `dispatch` and `workflow`, all three required when the table is present (`ConfigRefusal::ReleaseIncomplete` names the missing one), and a project that declares no table answers `/release` with a pane bulletin — the slash registry is static, so per-project hiding is not available, and silence is the one outcome nobody can diagnose ([L31]). The table's `workflow` is the only part that reaches the deck, on the aggregate's `release` surface. **Every verb copies the commit landing's seams rather than inventing its own.** `/push` is `changeset_push`: payload parser, `handle_control` arm, `run_changeset_push` in `feeds/changeset.rs`, one server-formatted summary, a receipt row, `_ok`/`_err`, a store phase, a receipt-hook edge, and a receipt block claimed by its own matcher. It elects nothing — a push sends the branch the checkout is on — and `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0` is load-bearing, because the card's shell has no TTY ([D111]) and a git that decided to ask would hang with nothing on screen. The three release verbs are the same shape in `feeds/release.rs`. **The exit code is the verdict, never the rows.** `release_check` claims `FAIL`/`ok`/`note` lines into rows and keeps the whole merged transcript as `raw`, but `passed` is `exit_code == Some(0)`: a check that prints only `ok` lines and exits 1 is not blessed, and a check that prints nothing and exits 0 is. **The server re-checks before it dispatches**, so a stale green on screen cannot ship, and the override past a failing check is a confirmed one behind a popover whose default focus is Cancel — on a shade `defaultButtonOwnsReturn` deliberately does not reach, because widening it would put a real release one reflexive Return away. **One poll exists in the whole feature and it says when it gives up** ([L33]): a watcher per `(project_dir, run_id)` asks `gh run view` every few seconds, ends on completion, and at a 45-minute ceiling broadcasts a final frame reading `abandoned` rather than going quiet — a watch that merely stopped would leave the shade showing an in-flight step forever. `unreachable` and `abandoned` are reports about the *watch* and carry no steps, so the deck's store carries the last real frame's url and steps forward; the shade's elapsed counter is a DOM tick ([L06]), a second hand on a watch the server keeps. **The notes editor is a row mode the host owns.** A `release-notes/*.md` row in the Changes shade gains a pencil that swaps its diff body for a `TugTextCardEditor` over one `TextCardStore` in automatic-save mode — the Text card's substrate, not a second editor — and the conflict notice is a `TugInlineAlert` rather than a `TugPaneBanner`, because a pane banner portals into the card and sets `inert` on the pane body with no refcount, and the Session card already renders exactly one. *Do not re-propose*: a `passed` derived from the rows (the check's exit code is the only thing that can be right); a client-side poll for the run (the server watches it, and a frame for a project this client never asked about is still kept, because the run is real whoever asked); `release` as a route or a card rather than a shade view (the shade view controller is the Changes shade's family, which is where a release sheet belongs); or a Window-menu Release item, which would publish a third visibility bit nothing reads. `tugrust/crates/tugtool-core/src/config.rs` (`ReleaseConfig`), `tugrust/crates/tugcast-core/src/types.rs` (`ReleaseSurface`), `tugrust/crates/tugcast/src/feeds/release.rs` (the three verbs, the check grammar, the watcher), `feeds/changeset.rs` (`run_changeset_push`, `format_push_summary`), `feeds/agent_supervisor.rs` (the arms), `feeds/changeset_all.rs` (the aggregate's `release`); `tugdeck/src/lib/release-store.ts`, `lib/shade-view-controller.ts`, `lib/slash-commands.ts`, `components/tugways/cards/session-release/session-release-view.tsx` + `.css`, `cards/session-changes/session-changes-notes-editor.tsx` + `.css`, `components/tugways/tug-changes-list.tsx`, `lib/file-read-error-copy.ts`, `cards/session-card.tsx`; `.claude/skills/release-tug/`; `Justfile` (`bless` reads origin through `gh api`; `just release --yes`). [D111], [D116], [D171], [L06], [L22], [L26], [L31], [L33], [tracking-changes.md](tracking-changes.md#the-landing-workflow)
